L1491: ## Reviewer Comment
L1492: The Sponsor used a bracketing approach for sWFI PFS. The manufacturer's (b) (4) experience with sWFI and the validation data available for 1.5 mL (b) (4) syringe format (note that the 1.5 mL is the only validation data used for the DP) were utilized by the Sponsor. The Sponsor's specification for the sWFI PFS fill volumes of (b) (4) mL and (b) (4) mL fall within (b) (4) validation data. However, the fill volume of (b) (4) mL is a little lower than (b) (4) mL validated range. Therefore, the Sponsor provided supplemental validation batch (VOTD18) with a target fill volume of (b) (4) mL. Although the Sponsor added a supplemental validation batch for the lower fill volume, it is still acceptable for the Sponsor to leverage (b) (4) mL fill volume because of its history. Since the minimum extractable volume of the Sponsor's (b) (4) mL diluent is slightly lower ( (b) (4) mL difference), it was therefore concluded that (b) (4). With (b) (4) validation and supplemental validation performed by the Sponsor the results demonstrated that the extractable volume met the pre-defined acceptance criteria for the 2 DP concentration of either 0.8 mg/mL or 2 mg/mL post-reconstitution that are applicable to the (b) (4) dosages/strength presentation of the DP (0.4 mg/vial, 0.56 mg/vial, 1.2 mg/vial (b) (4)). Despite (b) (4) validation data for the sWFI, in order to fulfill the Design Control requirements per 21 CFR 820.30, the Sponsor performed verification testing to ensure that (b) (4) WFI PFS functions adequately with the components in the co-package combination product such as compatibility with luer connections (e.g., sWFI PFS with the diluent needle). The design verification is in Table 3.2.R.2.5.1 above.
L1493: It is noted:
L1494: - extractable volume/volume in container is part of regular release testing and stability
- container closure integrity test (Dye Leak Test), is not part of regular release testing, but part of stability and release testing was done in the validation batches only
- BL/GF are not part of regular release testing and release testing was done in the validation batches only
- The Sponsor also performed other functional performance test (e.g., luer compatibility [sWFI + diluent needle]) with 90% confidence and 90% confidence in which the methodologies are FDA recognized.
- The focused of this review is to ensure that the essential performance requirements (dose accuracy, BL/GF, injection force, needle safety feature) meet a 95% confidence and 95% reliability.
L1495: Dose Accuracy: acceptable
L1496: - The Sponsor provided validation testing for the fill volume for the sWFI PFS to ensure that an appropriate amount is extruded in the DP vial for reconstitution. In addition to the above testing, a withdrawal volume assessment was also performed to evaluate that the fill volumes (per USP &lt;697&gt;) are adequate to withdraw the label claim volumes (3.2.P.2.2.1.6)
- *The test method is performed according to the current Ph. Eur., the current USP-NF and the current JP as described in the ICH Q4B Annex 2 harmonized Tripartite Guideline 'Test for Extractable Volume of Parenteral Preparations General Chapter'.*
- A Dose accuracy verification with the 510(k) cleared syringe (b) (4) was also performed, please note that the administration syringe is a 1 mL (b) (4) syringe with graduation appropriate for the various weight base dosing of the reconstituted DP. It is noted that the Sponsor only performed attribute testing with a sample size of n=22. This is not acceptable. An IR is sent to the Sponsor on 11/16/2020.
- On 12/17/2020, the Sponsor provided a response, in which the Sponsor analyzed the data as variable with the appropriate graphical summary and sample size justification. Please see section 6.2 for the complete response information.
- the provided data is acceptable
L1497: Break loose / Glide Force (BL/GF) for the sWFI: acceptable
L1498: - sWFI per (b) (4)
L1499: - the Sponsor provided a comparison of BL/GF with an empty syringe and PFS with sWFI, the results are comparable and met specifications of max. breakloose force (b) (4) and mean glide force (b) (4)
- the provided data is acceptable
L1500: Injection Force with the reconstituted drug product (DP) (not to exceed (b) (4): acceptable
L1501: - Design input Requirement (DIR) that demonstrate that when reconstituted DP is administered to the patient that it does not exceed (b) (4). The defined specification of (b) (4) is acceptable
- N=22 not acceptable, the sample size is not adequate for attribute testing because, the Agency typically requires a 95% confidence and 95% reliability with PFS. An IR will be sent to the Sponsor for Mid-Cycle.
- The IR response was received on 12/17/2020, in the response, the Sponsor performed an attribute testing with n=59. All samples met the acceptable criteria. Therefore, the response is acceptable.
L1502: Needle Safety feature: acceptable
L1503: - n=59 is acceptable, for needle Safety feature per the FDA Guidance, "Supplementary Guidance on Premarket Notifications for Medical Devices with Sharps Injury Prevention Features", https://www.fda.gov/media/71142/download
- it is noted that MAUDE search was also performed to cross reference any device issues with the 510(k) cleared devices used in this application
- (b) (4) clearance provided a simulated use study for the safety feature with 500 sample size. The sponsor performed attribute testing with a sample size of n=59 (DVTR-240004 report) and the 510(k) clearance simulated use testing is adequate to support the needle safety feature of the diluent needle. Therefore, it is acceptable.
- (b) (4) clearance predates the simulated used testing for the needle safety feature, therefore, this syringe did not have any simulated use testing for the safety feature with 500 sample size. However, a MAUDE search was performed to ensure that the syringe in this application is safe and effective.Based on the search criteria below (Product Code: (b) (4)) Report date: 11/1/2010 – 1/4/2021), there are only 6 reports related to the specific syringe (b) (4) being used in this application. The focus of the MAUDE search is if there are needles being left in the patient's tissue. It is noted that none of the reports pertains to needle being left in the patient.
- The Sponsor also performed Safety Actuation Performance with the reconstituted DP and the syringe and there are no deviation reported in the n=59 sample size per DVTR-240005 report
- Therefore, since there are no major issues with this model, I do not have any issues with the syringe not having the simulated use testing for the safety feature from its clearance.
- There are no recalls found with the 510(k) cleared devices included in this application

CDRH sent Device Performance Deficiency or Interactive Review Questions to the Sponsor:  Yes  No
L1517: Date Sent:
11/16/2020	Date/Sequence Received:
12/11/2020
Information Request #1	You provided verification testing for the functional performance for dose accuracy (DVTR-240003) and injection force for the assembled diluent needle and sWFI PFS (DVTR-240004) and reconstituted administration syringe (DVTR-240005). You performed attribute testing with the sample size n=22 for the dose accuracy and injection force. The functions tested are critical elements of the devices in the co-package combination product. The Agency typically accepts 95% confidence and 95% reliability to ensure that these essential performance function as intended. Therefore, the provided attribute testing with n=22 is not acceptable. Provide the following:Dose Accuracy – provide attribute testing with a 95% confidence and 95% reliability (e.g. sample size, n=59)
Injection force – provide attribute testing for assembled (a)diluent syringe and sWFI PFS and (b)administration syringe (with the reconstituted drug product or representative fluid) with a 95% confidence and 95% reliability for each (a and b)
Alternatively, analyze your data as variable with graphical summary with sample size justification

Sponsor Response	
In this response, additional administration syringe injection force testing data (n=59) are provided. Further, as suggested by the Agency, for administration syringe dose accuracy and assembled diluent needle and sWFI PFS injection force, data has been analyzed as variable with graphical summary with sample size justification. K-factor analyses performed per the appropriate ISO standards (ISO-16269-6, Statistical interpretation of data- Part 6: Determination of the Statistical Tolerance Intervals) demonstrate 95% confidence and 95% reliability.
Injection Force - Administration Syringe
As requested by the Agency, BioMarin is providing additional data in accordance with 95% confidence and 95% reliability for the administration syringe injection force attribute testing. The additional data demonstrate that the administration syringe achieved the injection force requirement with a 95% confidence and 95% reliability.
In administration syringe design verification report (DVTR-240005), injection force and needle safety feature actuation were tested in conjunction since the syringe's plunger must be completely depressed to activate the safety mechanism. The needle retraction safety mechanism was tested at 95/95% confidence/reliability due to higher risk to patients per the
L1518: internal risk assessment. Testing was done with 59 reconstituted drug product samples for both injection force and needle retraction safety mechanism. However, only results for the first 22 samples were provided as required for the 90/90% confidence/reliability as specified in ISO-16269-6, Statistical interpretation of data- Part 6: Determination of the Statistical Tolerance Intervals.
L1519: Table 1 summarizes the complete data set of all 59 samples testing the administration syringe injection force. The injection force for all 59 samples measured well below the N acceptance criteria with an average of 3.9 N and a standard deviation of 0.65. The administration syringe achieved the injection force requirement with 95/95% confidence/reliability.
